--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'll analyze the aggregation mission carefully.

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region (the BLACK-floored strip bordered by BLUE walls)
- Target: get all robots into the BLACK-floored strip near BLUE walls
- Robots start in the center (GRAY floor)

**Key perceptions:**
- Floor color: BLACK = target strip or other strip, GRAY = middle zone
- Wall colors: BLUE = target side, GREEN = other side
- The robot camera can see colored objects (walls count)

**Strategy:**
- Robots should move toward BLUE walls (GoToColor with blue=3? Let me check: colors {1,2,3,4,5,6} - blue is likely 3)
- When on BLACK floor (could be either strip), need to distinguish: if robot sees BLUE walls it's in the right strip, if it sees GREEN walls it should leave
- GoAwayColor from GREEN (color 2?) walls, GoToColor toward BLUE walls (color 3?)

**State design:**
- State 0: GoToColor BLUE (color 3) - move toward blue walls
- State 1: Exploration - when lost, explore to find blue walls
- State 2: Stop - when on BLACK floor AND seeing BLUE (stay put)

Let me design 14 machines with varying strategies:

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I need to see the results from Round 2 to know which machines performed best. Since no scores were provided, I'll reason from first principles and diversify my approach.

**Re-examining the mission:**
- Target: BLACK-floored strip bordered by BLUE walls
- The other strip is also BLACK but bordered by GREEN walls
- GRAY floor = middle zone (bad, robots should leave)
- BLACK floor = either target (good) or wrong strip (bad)

**Key insight:** 
- GoToColor with BLUE (color 3) moves toward blue walls → correct direction
- GoAwayColor from GREEN (color 2) also helps
- When on BLACK + seeing BLUE walls → STOP (score 0 contribution)
- When on BLACK + seeing GREEN walls → need to escape!
- C=7 (perceives color) with blue=3 distinguishes good vs bad black strip

**Refined strategy:**
- State "GoToBlue": move toward blue walls
- State "Stop": when on black floor AND perceiving blue walls
- State "Escape": when on black floor but seeing green walls, go away from green

Let me note color codes: looking at the format, clr values {1..6} likely map to red=1, green=2, blue=3, yellow=4, magenta=5, cyan=6.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I still haven't received explicit scores from the rounds. I need to reason carefully and diversify more aggressively.

**Re-examining the problem:**

The mission says "AGGREGATION" but the scoring is about robots being in the target BLACK strip (BLUE walls). Wait - re-reading: "score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region." This is actually a **go-to-region** task, not classic aggregation.

**Critical re-check of color codes:**
- Blue walls border the target strip. In ARGoS, colored walls are perceived by the camera as objects. Color 3 = blue seems right.
- Green walls = color 2? Or maybe different mapping. Let me try both.

**What I know works conceptually:**
- GoToColor(blue) should steer robots toward the blue-walled strip
- On BLACK floor + seeing blue = good, stay/stop
- On BLACK floor + seeing green = wrong strip, escape
- On GRAY floor = middle, keep moving toward blue

**New ideas to try:**
- Using FlockCenter to aggregate robots already in the strip
- Using spiral to cover ground faster
- Simpler 1-state machines (just GoToColor blue always)
- Different probability thresholds

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm not receiving explicit scores, I need to think more carefully about what might be going wrong and try fundamentally different approaches.

**Deep re-analysis:**

The mission title says "AGGREGATION" but the scoring is about being in the BLACK strip near BLUE walls. Wait - let me re-read: "score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region." This is a coverage/homing task.

**What might be failing:**
1. Color indices might be wrong - blue walls might not be color 3
2. The GoToColor behavior might not work as expected near walls
3. Robots might get stuck oscillating

**Alternative color mappings to try:**
- Maybe blue=4, green=5? Or other orderings
- Try ALL color options for GoToColor to hedge bets

**Key insight I may have missed:** The floor is BLACK in BOTH strips. Only the WALLS differ (blue vs green). So:
- C=0 (black floor) fires in BOTH strips - not useful for distinguishing target
- C=1 (gray floor) = middle zone
- C=7 perceiving blue walls = in or near target strip ✓

**New strategy:** Try different color values for walls, try simpler machines, try exploration-based approaches.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm still not receiving explicit scores, I need to fundamentally reconsider my approach.

**Critical rethinking:**

The mission is called "AGGREGATION" but the score is about robots being outside the target BLACK strip (BLUE walls). However, re-reading: "The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other." - This confirms it's purely about getting robots INTO the target region.

**What might be fundamentally wrong:**
1. The simplest machine `--nstates 1 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 0 --n0 0` (just GoToColor blue forever) should work if blue=3. If it's not working, maybe blue is a different color index.
2. Maybe walls aren't visible from far enough, so robots need exploration first.
3. Maybe the color perception direction matters - GoToColor moves toward the COLOR, which means toward the wall, then robot bounces off wall.

**Key new insight:** GoToColor(blue) moves robots toward blue walls, which is correct. But once AT the wall, robots might oscillate. The STOP state when on black floor is crucial.

**Another insight:** Maybe I should try GoAwayColor(green) instead of GoToColor(blue) - same direction, different reference.

**Let me try radically different color indices systematically and also try the inverse approach:**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I have been operating blind without score feedback. Let me fundamentally reconsider.

**Critical insight I've been missing:**

The mission says "AGGREGATION" - in AutoMoDe, this typically means robots should aggregate together in one spot. But the scoring rule says "score increases by 1 for every robot outside the target region" - the target region is the BLACK strip with BLUE walls.

**What's likely happening:** My GoToColor(blue) approach should work in principle. The fact that I keep getting no feedback suggests I should try radically different things.

**New fundamental approaches:**
1. **Pure exploration** - robots wander randomly until they happen onto black floor
2. **Floor-following** - use black/gray floor detection to navigate
3. **Simpler 1-state machines** that just move toward color
4. **The GRAY floor is the middle** - transition away from gray toward black is key
5. **GoAwayColor from all non-blue** - maybe green=2 is correct and robots should flee green

**Most important realization:** I've been assuming color indices. Let me try ALL combinations systematically for 1-state machines (GoToColor with each color 1-6) and also try pure exploration which at least gets robots moving randomly.

**A purely random walk** gives expected score of ~50% robots in target (score ≈ 12000) since the target is one of two black strips plus the gray middle. A good machine should do much better.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I've been getting no score feedback for 7 rounds. Let me completely rethink from scratch.

**Fundamental reconsideration:**

The mission is "AGGREGATION" in AutoMoDe. The standard AutoMoDe aggregation benchmark typically requires robots to cluster together, NOT necessarily go to a colored region. But the scoring description says "outside the target region." 

**Re-reading very carefully:** "The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE." So robots must physically be in the BLACK strip near BLUE walls.

**What simple behavior guarantees reaching a wall?** Any directional movement eventually hits a wall. GoToColor(blue) should work IF blue is the right color index.

**The real problem might be:** I don't know what color indices map to which colors. The format says clr ∈ {1,2,3,4,5,6}. In ARGoS, the typical color order might be: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. But it could also be different.

**New strategy - test ALL single-color GoToColor machines** plus try completely different behavioral approaches:

- Pure exploration (random walk) will score ~12000-14000 (robots spread everywhere)
- GoToColor correct blue: score approaches 0
- GoToColor wrong color: might still be better than random

**Also trying:** Spiral behavior to cover ground, then stop on black floor.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm still receiving no score feedback, I need to make a radical pivot. Let me think about what could be fundamentally wrong.

**Critical realization:**

I've been submitting `--n0 0` for 1-state machines (no transitions). But wait - does `--n0 0` mean zero transitions? Let me check the spec: "number of transitions leaving state I, integer in [1..4]" - it says [1..4], meaning MINIMUM 1! A state with zero transitions may crash or be invalid.

So my 1-state machines with `--n0 0` have been CRASHING the simulator! That's why I get no useful feedback - those machines are invalid.

**Fix:** Every state needs at least 1 transition. For a "stay in this state" behavior, I need a self-loop - but rule 4 says "Never point a transition at its own state." 

So the minimum valid machine needs at least 2 states. State 0 transitions to state 1 and state 1 transitions back to state 0.

**Rebuilt strategy from scratch:**

- 2-state machines: GoToColor(blue) with a self-returning loop via the other state
- The simplest effective design: GoToColor(blue) → on black floor → Stop → on gray floor → GoToColor(blue)

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
**Final round - must make it count.**

After 9 rounds with no score feedback, I need to accept I don't know which machines performed best. But I've learned:

1. `--n<I> 0` is likely INVALID (spec says [1..4]) - my 1-state machines were probably crashing
2. The 2-state GoToColor+Stop machines should be valid and functional
3. I've been systematically trying all color indices (1-6) for GoToColor

**What I know about the problem:**
- Robots need to reach BLACK floor near BLUE walls
- GRAY floor = middle (bad)
- BLACK floor = either strip (need to distinguish)
- Blue walls are the target marker

**For the final round, I'll:**
1. Keep the best-candidate 2-state machines (all color variants - hedging on color index)
2. Use stronger 3-state machines with escape from wrong strip
3. Add machines with higher stop-probability (stay put when on black floor)
4. Include exploration-first machines

**Key design:** GoToColor(X) → on black → Stop; Stop → on gray → GoToColor(X). This ensures robots that reach black floor stay there.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
